# The Dig: the sinkhole (`park_sinkhole`, stage 4)

Stage 4 greybox round 4, 13 October 2026. Traversal qualification passes on the committed tool (park ruling 28): batch, solo and traced runs agree and all 56 seed rows reach the Warden (section 9). That round's claim that every band holds and that there were zero stops was later superseded: the 2026-10-19 head and base batches in the traversal doc both miss `segment_s` and `dead_stops` for some pilots. The shared cave-in effect still raises a mound instead of the authored trench; the natural-event owner fixes it in a separate unit.

2026-10-19: the sinkhole's greybox rows are dressed. `lava_channel_1..21`, `west_trench`, `south_flats` and `lava_chute` now carry GLB recipes (`lava_channel`, `lava_trench_420x6x40`, `dry_wash_1500x4x60`, `lava_chute_3x0p5x8`) with `"fit": "seat"`, matching `src/world/park/park_sinkhole.json`. `lava_channel` is draped on the baked ground (`DRAPED` in `park_recipes.gd`): before it the centre line ran from +3.2 m above ground to -2.1 m below, after it the centre sits +0.16 to +0.23 m on 19 of 21 rows (ch_9 +0.45, ch_20 +0.61 to -0.15) and the highest drawn vertex in the lane is +0.21 m; the lips at the crest edge were still mixed (ch_13 +3.9, ch_20 +2.9) until the crest band and the beyond-crest prop end zones went into `lavachannels.py` (lips now +0.21 to +0.60 on 20 rows, ch_20 +1.17; zero rim-prop vertices in a neighbour's lane, was 2,254). `lava_chute` is fully buried, about 1 m under the terrain. Open follow-up (owner: World, card to be filed by the coordinator): the row carries ride_fact `ride_chute` but sits about 1 m under the terrain at y -85.19, as its greybox did (see the `lava_chute` row in the JSON block below); re-datum it to the ground or drop it. Tables are in [the traversal doc](../traversal/park_sinkhole.md). For Andrew's play pass: each `lava_channel` row's rim props now cover about 36% of the piece (roughly row metres 16 to 35), so expect bare stretches of 20 m or more between consecutive rows' dressing along the chain.

2026-10-22: the bend post `lava_channel_post_21i` moves 7 m outward across `lava_channel_16` (east-northeast), from (-164.9, -448.2) to (-158.3, -450.6), off the inside of the 16/17 bend. Its sheer +20 m stamp stood about 2 m off the wall at the 16/17 bend, and a ball landing from `tube_rope_1` and hugging the inside of the bend struck it at (-172.8, -446.2) (ground rise 25.5 m over an 8 m disc, now 5.7 m), then dead-stopped on the junction floor 1.5 s later at (-171, -439). Guarded by the bend check in `tests/park_cable_ends_test.gd`. Rows 16 and 17 and the lava hazard are unchanged; the post still anchors the inner bank (its neighbours `lava_channel_post_20i` and `_22i` are 36 m and 14 m away).

2026-10-22: two more sheer anchor posts move outward off their washes. `main_tube_upper_post_11i` moves 8 m east, from (-35.3, -120.2) to (-27.3, -120.2): a corner stood 0.4 m inside `main_tube_upper_8`'s footprint (clear by 6.8 m now), and Sable dead-stopped against it at (-40.8, -114.4) on 5 of 8 seeds in a trace tree whose sinkhole data equals stage 42ed00ee's and 2 of 8 on 0446a2f2 (ground rise 26.0 m over an 8 m disc, now 6.0 m). `lava_channel_post_13i` moves 6.7 m east-northeast, from (-313.1, -585.2) to (-307.1, -582.2): it stood 2.0 m off `lava_channel_10` (now 6.9 m) and Sable seed 8 struck it airborne and stopped at (-318.7, -588.4) (rise 22.3 m, now 4.7 m). Guarded by the strike checks in `tests/park_cable_ends_test.gd`. Washes, channels and lava are unchanged; both posts keep their yaw and stay bank anchors.

2026-10-23: the lava channel's elbows A (`lava_channel_12`>`13`) and B (`lava_channel_20`>`21`) are eased (World, #241 6354292643 item 3), by reshaping rows 12, 13 and 21 only. Row 12 `at` (-238.6, -605.1) to (-237.3, -606.7), yaw 68.7 to 60.8, length 59.28 to 59.9; row 13 `at` (-203.4, -580.1) to (-202.4, -580.2), yaw 40.5 to 42.5, length 59.35 to 55.6; row 21 `at` (-13.0, -384.0) to (-11.1, -383.7), yaw 30.6 to 31.6, length 51.16 to 50.7 (height and width unchanged; row 21's far end moves from (0.0, -362.0) to (2.2, -362.1), 4.0 m to 4.7 m from `tube_mouth`; `crust_kicker` and row 20 are untouched). The excess was a bank notch at the outer corner of each junction (the end of one wash sits at the other's wall edge while the floor beside it is full depth), not a doubled inner corner. `tests/park_wash_elbow_test.gd` now reads A at kink 18.3 deg and excess 1.08 m (was 28.2 and 1.55) and B at 19.2 and 0.95 (was 20.2 and 1.64); `lava_channel_11>12` (32.1 deg, 1.00) and `13>14` (32.2 deg, 1.07) fall under the 1.25 m bound as well; `14>15` and `19>20` stay flagged and are the test's positive controls. The summed wash depth on rows 12, 13 and 21 centre lines is now exactly the 4 m design (was up to 0.32 m deeper, on row 21), row 11 reads 0.25 m deeper (was 0.15) and no centre line goes more than 0.5 m below the design; the straight mid-run reads the same (2.71 m), and the floor over each edited joint is about 0.3 to 0.4 m shallower than before. The bake and graph are regenerated; the dressing pins (`park_dressing_pin_test`, `park_props_test` sinkhole expansion) wait for Dressing's re-pin.

2026-10-23 (second pass): the next two flagged lava channel elbows, `lava_channel_14`>`15` and `19`>`20`, are eased (World, #241 6356545723), by shortening rows 14 and 20 only: row 14 length 58.51 to 56.5 and row 20 length 59.4 to 55.0 (`at`, yaw, height and width unchanged; row 14's `tube_rope_1` to `4` cable sites and every other row are untouched). Shortening takes back the double dig where the two washes overlap: `tests/park_wash_elbow_test.gd` now reads `14>15` at kink 33.3 deg and excess 1.00 m (was 1.38) and `19>20` at 36.1 deg and 1.12 m (was 1.49), both under the 1.25 m bound, while `13>14` falls to 0.91 m (was 1.07) and `20>21` to 0.53 m (was 0.95, kink 19.2 deg unchanged); no other sinkhole elbow moves, so the flagged set drops from 15 to 13. The summed wash depth on the centre lines is now at most 0.16 m past the 4 m design (row 14 -4.16, was -4.28; row 20 -4.11, was -4.31; row 19 -4.11, was -4.35), and the floor over each touched joint is shallower than before by 0.18 m (`13>14`), 0.25 m (`14>15`), 0.39 m (`19>20`) and 0.40 m (`20>21`); `15>16` and `18>19` are unchanged. Sharper bends of the rows (the variants that move `at` or yaw) either dug the joint deeper, pushed `18>19` (kink 17.5 deg, excess 1.50 m) over the 18 deg kink bound, or moved row 20's far end to 0.9 m from `lava_channel_post_26i`; the plateaus near the two rows keep their gaps (nearest: `lava_channel_fill_625` 0.3 m from row 14, `lava_channel_fill_865` 1.0 m from row 20). The bake and graph are regenerated; no dressing pin moves (`park_dressing_pin_test` and `park_props_test` pass unchanged).

Read with [PARK.md](../../PARK.md) (the tables and bands), [TADR-011](../../../../../adr/TADR-011-park-terrain-and-warden-kinds.md) (the decision), the Warden kind sheet [wardens/swarm.md](../../wardens/swarm.md) and the stage 3 page [3-park_pit.md](3-park_pit.md), which defines bench, winze, collar and waymark. Paths are under `sketches/core-loop/` unless they start with `adr/`, `docs/` or `BIBLE.md`.

Terms used once and kept: a **lava tube** is a cave a lava river left when its crust cooled and the lava drained; a **skylight** is a hole where a tube's roof fell in; a **drift** is a level mine tunnel; a **crosscut** is a drift that joins two others; a **caisson** is a lined pump shaft, here flooded; the **gallery ring** is the timber walkway the miners built round the sinkhole at the 100 m mark.

## 1. Landform and society layer

**The Arizona landform.** The San Francisco and Uinkaret volcanic fields: black basalt flats, a cinder cone with a breached crater, and lava tubes under the plateau like the Lava River Cave near Flagstaff, 1 km of tube you can walk. Here the tubes ran south and drained into a karst sinkhole that later flooded, the way cenotes and collapse sinks form where rock dissolves under a cap. Miners found copper in the tube walls and drove drifts between the tubes; they sank a pump caisson in the sinkhole floor to keep the workings dry. The plateau's top is the stage's ceiling, so a run is mostly a ride through the dark between skylights.

**The park, in numbers (start).** Cinder cone centre (-320, -705), 460 m across, 90 m tall, its crater breached on the south-east. Lava channel 30 m wide and 8 m deep from the breach to the tube mouth at (0, -360). Five tubes and drifts from the tube mouth (y -12) fall to the great room, a cavern 90 m across at (0, -20), floor y -55. Three lower tubes fall from the great room to the sinkhole's gallery ring (y -100). Sinkhole rim radius 125 m at y 0; walls drop 100 m to the gallery ring (radius 40 to 55 m); the caisson pool sits inside it, radius 40 m, coping y -110, floor y -120.

**Who shaped it.**

| Society | What they left | Rows | Rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `none` | the cone, the lava channel, the tubes, the skylights, the great room, the sinkhole walls, the cenote, the west trench, the spatter field, the east mesa, the south flats | most rows | water, fire and time |
| `mining` | the east drift, the east haulage drift, the breach caps, the gallery ring, the caisson, the pump gallery | 6 families | carries homes and events freely |
| `ancestral_puebloan` | a row of small stone granaries in the west trench wall | `trench_granaries` | no home, no ground-changing event, `ref_` fragment only (PARK.md section 4) |
| `ancient` | a sealed vault off the cannon tube, where a buried machine woke | `machine_vault` | the ancient eruption's site |
| `fleet` | nothing at start; a drill barge lands on the east plateau at clock tier 2 and bores down | `bore_apron` | brings a dormant drill crew home |

The granaries sit in the trench wall off every measured line, a quiet side loop for a player who roams west. They are the stage's reference to people who stored seed against a dry year in this country, and nothing hostile lives in them.

## 2. The hero landmark and the Warden's mounting

**The sinkhole.** A round collapse 250 m across that opens the plateau to the sky. Its walls are layered basalt over pale limestone, streaked with mineral stain, with the miners' timber gallery ring at the 100 m mark and the caisson's flooded pool in the middle. From the plateau you cannot see into it, so the landmark is its light column: a uranium-glass green beam 4 m wide (start) rising from the nest to `sight_top` [0, 140, 388], 140 m above the plain. The column is the brood's light, not daylight, so it reads as "the Warden is there" and never as a floor telegraph (section 8).

**The mounting (`sinkhole`).** The brood nests on the sinkhole's south wall at [0, -99, 388], over the pool's south edge, 21 m above the pool floor. It does not move (`warden.path` is empty). Its guard is a lamp swarm, as today's `brood` variant already makes it (`src/combat/warden.gd:31-32`, `225-228`). The mounting's one mechanism is PARK.md's (PARK.md:427): **the pool rises one step per chunk lost**, 5 m a step (start), lifting the fight floor toward the nest: floor y -120, then -115, -110, -105. The break floods the pool to the gallery ring (y -100), and the bay docks over the water. The kind sheet ([swarm.md](../../wardens/swarm.md) section 3.2) holds the full rule, including the hold that keeps a step from rising under the ball.

**Fight floor.** The pool's floor is a shallow bowl under 1.2 m of water at most, so a ball rolls through and keeps planar speed (PARK.md:294). The gallery ring is a 15 m wide timber shelf all the way round, a wall ride that circles the pool. A ball carves the ring, drops off its inner edge onto the pool floor for a ram or a slam, and climbs back out by the pump-gallery ramp (after the boiler blows), the rim zip's end, a harpoon swing, or the pulse's 10 m/s lift (`warden.gd:42`).

**Bay and gates.** Gate slot (0, 305) on the gallery ring's north side, facing -z toward the arriving ball. The bay docks at (0, 320), 15 m from the gate and inside the gates' 110 m hold (`src/world/bay.gd:21-23`).

The camp sits on every line, in the one room every tube passes through (R2). The hauler bounty waits in a side chamber off the room's east wall, its door 164 m of path from the line; the cracked cache in the west chamber, 224 m off, is the reward (PARK.md 2.12, R1, R7).

**Breach plates become caps.** Today's deep works carries five breach plates (`src/world/map_dig_deep.gd:17-18`, `60`). Here two mining caps keep the slam verb: `upper_tube_cap` on the main tube drops 17 m into the cannon tube's tail, and `great_room_cap` drops 15 m from the great room onto the main lower tube. The cap and its `drop_to` need the schema key the stage 3 page proposes (its section 14, question 1).

**Anchors for the 40 m gap rule.** Tube recipes carry basalt pillars or lava stalactites every 30 m (start); drifts carry timber sets every 6 m; the gallery ring carries its posts every 12 m. The spatter field's hoodoos and the cone's lip blocks cover the surface lines.

**Density gaps for KD4.** Column A and the far east (G3, H6 to H8) sit more than 80 m from any footprint. KD4 fills them with `ground` rows or widens `west_trench`, `east_mesa` and `south_flats`.

**As shipped (KD4 greybox, 2026-10-11).** This section is the draft. The shipped file has 504 park rows: the tube families are resampled into straight segments of about 50 to 60 m, each lined with basalt posts and fillers, and each tube carries a kicker and a lava vent (section 7). `ground` rows do not count for density (PARK.md section 8), so the last three gaps are spatter fields on the plateau: `cone_skirt_spatter` (-200, -770), `channel_bank_spatter` (-345, -330) and `east_plateau_spatter` (280, -10); `park_density_test` then reads worst 74.0 m, none over 80 m.

**Fix round (KD4, 2026-10-12).** 496 park rows. Eight filler blocks that closed the island between the three lower tubes where they meet the gallery ring (z 257 to 267, x -15 to 19) are gone: a ball rolling straight from the great room at the Warden wedged on them at (0, 255). Every row but the ground now carries a recipe (R10): the stamped rows carry `greybox:<element>`, which places a node and, where the row has a ride fact, its sensor, while the stamp stays the solid (`park_recipes.gd` `stamp_replaces`); the anchor-field rows (the basalt posts and fillers and the seven spatter and hoodoo fields) carry `greybox:anchor_field`, so they build harpoon posts at runtime. Rows left on recipe `""`, with the reason:

- `great_room`, `east_chamber`, `west_chamber`, `crosscut_drift`, `west_cenote`, `sinkhole_walls`, `caisson_bowl`: site markers for an activity, event, cracked wall or the Warden, with no stamp and a height of 0. A greybox box of the row's size would be a zero-height solid; the ground under them is the stamps around them.
- The ten kicker rows (`cinder_kicker`, `crust_kicker` and one per tube): each one's paired `<id>_ramp` ground row stamps the lip, and the traversal tool reads the lip from the park map (`near_kicker_lip`). A greybox kicker wedge would stack a second 2.5 m lip on the stamped one.
- The ground rows, by R10's own scope.

**Full ring (storyboard 4 call B, 2026-10-17).** Andrew picked the full gallery ring on 16 Oct (`storyboards/DECISIONS.md`). The 70 m strip at y -108.24, which sat buried about 8 m under the -100 pit floor so its sensor never fired, is replaced by 24 ring rows, `gallery_ring` and `gallery_ring_2` to `_24`: 15 m wide from radius 46 to 61 round the pool centre (0, 350), each a zero-height `plateau` ledge, so the bake paints the band and keeps the floor and the row places only its `ride_ledge` sensor (`stamp_replaces`). The greybox pit's south wall stood at z 400, 10 m behind the pool, so `gallery_cut_1` to `_15` cut it back to radius 66 between x -45 and 45 and the ring passes behind the nest. `pump_gallery` moves to (73, 350), outside the lap, and route `gallery_lap` runs round it. `tools/park_ring_rows.py` writes the rows. Re-measured in the cloud on 8 seeds against `cf63e622` measured the same way: all six pilots and the floor reach the Warden 8/8 with zero dead stops, arrival medians move by at most 1.5 s (floor 99.44 s, worst 100.44 s), and the completionist win proof clears stage 4 on seeds 1 to 3. Cloud numbers differ slightly from the round-4 table above (another machine and a later main), so compare like with like.

**Real basin (Andrew, 2026-10-17).** Andrew tapped "Real basin" and then asked for the pool to be a bowl. Row `caisson_pit`, a `bowl` stamp 80 m across and 20 m deep at (0, 350), sinks the pool floor from the -100 pit floor to -120. The swarm's pool slab now builds there and rises 5 m per chunk lost (`src/combat/warden_swarm.gd`). Under the nest the ground is about -102. A straight-walled `sinkhole` stamp was tried first. It hung the nest over a 20 m wall that a ball on the pool floor could neither ram nor climb, and the completionist bot stalled on 2 of 3 seeds. Re-measured in the cloud against the ring-only `1548fa8f`, completionist, stage limit 1500 s:
- Every pilot and the floor still reach the Warden 8/8 with zero dead stops. The floor median is 97.24 s, worst 100.37 s.
- Stage 4 clears on 5 of 6 seeds, as before; seed 5 dies at the same tick on both trees.
- The fight is slower: clear times by seed run 426, 451, 671, 1297 and 351 s against 235, 408, 455, 569 and 297 s.
- Two things are still open. `park_graph`'s `beacon_ok` wants a disc of `reach_m` round the beacon that is flat within 2.5 m, which a 20 m basin cannot meet, so `park_park_sinkhole_test` fails that pin until the rule judges a mounting pool by its slab. And line fire from the pool floor builds about 0.1 posture a second, so the 45 to 70 s first stagger predicted above comes from rams instead.

### Authored event proof, round 4

KE-FORMS is imported through STEP 0; every authored event row remains byte-identical to round 3. The completionist seed-1 stage run uses the live director and natural handler, with a read-only observer in the round folder. The receipt (`kd4-sinkhole-r4/event-proof.json`) verifies registration, announcement, landing, the installed cave-in swap and the drain effect, with no unsupported-form error. The cave-in's depth and fill timing fail the separate check below.

- `west_roof_cave_in` announces at 45.01 s, with 6.61 s remaining, and lands at 51.64 s. Its warning `ring` and sound `threat_tick` and landed `burst` and sound `slam_boom` read the shared records. Announcement and landing log (`kd4-sinkhole-r4/win-1.log`) lines 114 and 154. The committed `west_collapse_trench_after` overlay is installed. Trigger tier 1, lead 6 s, 12 m basalt swap, authored fill 3 s, permanent live row and hold-clear 20 m remain unchanged.
- `cenote_drain` announces at 122.62 s, with 5.75 s remaining, and lands at 128.37 s. Its warning `chevron` and sound `boost_whoosh` and landed `chevron` and sound `found_chime` read the shared records. Announcement and landing log (`kd4-sinkhole-r4/win-1.log`) lines 957 and 960. Water starts at the authored 1.2 m and reaches 0 m after 12 s; rendered and queried depth agree. Trigger tier 3, lead 5 s, live 60 s, hold-clear 12 m and cooldown 120 s remain unchanged.

This is headless sight/sound wiring and gameplay-state evidence. It does not establish a visual or listening verdict.

**Remaining shared natural-effect failure.** The cave-in's authored 12 m trench over 3 s is not implemented by this merged runtime. At its centre [-205, 250], the live terrain height rises from 1.36363 m to 12.31474 m at landing, a 10.95111 m rise. It is already unchanged at both 1.5 s and 3 s after landing. The independent effect check (`kd4-sinkhole-r4/cave-effect-proof.json`) is FAIL. `events/event_ground.gd` uses the fixed Gorge rubble profile for cave_in, and `events/natural.gd` advances fill only for water and collapse. The shared event owner must repair the cave_in effect. KD4 keeps the authored row, lead, trigger, effect and hold rules; no substitution or stage geometry workaround is made. The event table describes authored intent, not a passing terrain-effect result.

## 8. Weather and time of day

**Time: `afternoon`, fixed.** The sun stands in the south-west. On the surface it rakes the cone and the spatter field with long shadows. Underground it enters only through the skylights and the sinkhole, so the tubes read as dark with bright holes. The `world_look.gd` `TIME_ROWS` afternoon row owns sun height and colour (unit KE7).

| Id | Kind | Trigger | Live | Fog | Sky, sound, layer | Event odds |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `tube_mist` | cave_mist | always | always | density 0.004, `#9fb3b8` | clear; `cave_drip`; `drip_keys` | none |
| `afternoon_monsoon` | monsoon | clock tier 2, lead 8 s | 45 s, gap 120 s | density 0.008, `#6f7d86` | `storm_build`; `monsoon_rain`; `rain_toms` | `cenote_drain` x0.5 (rain refills the cenote) |

Both rows carry `handling: "none"`. The monsoon changes the view and the sound: rain curtains through the skylights and thin waterfalls down the sinkhole walls. It never changes the handling.

**The Dig lamps veto, kept.** VX8 (`364e1e6`, on Andrew's wave 11 veto list, `design-v3/HANDOFF.md:17`) removed the amber lamp pools and the breach-plate daylight shaft because they read as false telegraphs. This stage keeps that rule underground, where it matters most:

- Skylights get no volumetric light shaft and no light pool. They show the sky colour through the hole and raise the ambient level in a 20 m radius (start), nothing more.
- No warm OmniLight3D anywhere; drift lamps keep white, unpooled lenses (`src/presentation/dig_scenery.gd:404-405`).
- Breach caps carry no light under them.
- The only coloured light on a floor is a telegraph, a lamp swarm's lure bloom (an enemy's own read) or the brood's green.
- The light column is vertical, 4 m wide, centred on the nest and the Warden's colour. It lights no floor patch along a line.

## 9. Traversal lines and measurements

### Wall pays and the floor is lava, 2026-10-17

Andrew tapped "Wall pays" (22:37Z) and asked for the floor to be lava (23:24Z): touching the bottom is free, lingering hurts. The rule lives in `src/world/cinder_heat.gd`, and every number is a constant there.

- Contact on a cinder strip, with the ball on the floor by the Dig's floor rule, builds dwell. The first 1.5 s are free; the cinders glow from 0.6 s.
- After the grace it burns 4 hull per second in 0.5 s pulses, with a sizzle when the burn starts. It never takes the last point of hull.
- Leaving the floor or landing a hit on an enemy cools it at once, with a hiss and a steam puff, so bowling through a pack or zigzagging never burns.
- The strips also carry a light drag (0.08 per second of floor speed). Heat alone left the floor line as fast as the walls.

- The polish band up every tube wall is glaze (`tube_glaze`): a ball carving it gets a 7 m/s/s push along its travel, between 4 and 36 m/s. With #61's wall grip, carving lost speed against the floor. Before the glaze, every pilot's old floor route beat its wall line by 3 to 18 s.

One seed per pilot, `--table=park --stage=4 --seeds=1`, fixed-fps 90, on this branch with the wall grip from #61 merged. `lines.json` is now the carving lines: every waypoint in a tube sits at 0.81 of the half width on one wall. Kest, Cannon and Tally ride the travel's right wall, Tarn the left, and Sable and Rivet the outside of each bend. The old routes, which are mostly floor, stay as `lines_floor_routes.json` for this comparison. Times are to the Warden in seconds. Carve is the share of grounded ticks on a bank, wall or lip. Repeat runs of one line vary by up to 2.6 s.

| Pilot | Carving line | Carve | Old floor route | Carve | Floor burns (hull) |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Cannon | 88.9 | 0.40 | 95.9 | 0.02 | 9 (94) |
| Tarn | 91.6 | 0.43 | 94.2 | 0.02 | 3 (52) |
| Kest | 94.4 | 0.33 | 103.6 | 0.02 | 13 (80) |
| Tally | 97.3 | 0.34 | 98.9 | 0.01 | 5 (52) |
| Rivet | 102.1, 99.9 | 0.40 | 101.0, 101.0 | 0.01 | 0 |
| Sable | 66.9, 69.7 | 0.24 | 62.8, 65.8 | 0.04-0.06 | 2 (0-8) |
| Floor line (Rivet, `--floor`) | | | 104.8 | 0.02 | 11 (106) |

- Carving wins for Cannon, Kest, Tarn and Tally, and it ties for Rivet. No carving line took heat; every floor rider but Rivet burned.
- Sable is the exception. Sable is airborne half the run (0.48), so the ground barely matters, and its floor route is 3-4 s quicker. A glaze cap of 48 m/s did not change that.
- The tested alternatives were each slower: inside-of-bend wall lines, wall time only at the ropes and vents, and a glaze push of 4.
- With heat only and no glaze, the floor line tied the walls at 100 s, so the light drag stays.
- The floor bot never fights, so its burn is the worst case.


## Qualified round-4 measurement, 2026-10-13

Runtime inputs match STEP 0 merge `e3ae572401729243688f26deb47c60a9fe9f3b64`, carrying candidate `3debeb8`, TRAV-DET2 and KE-FORMS. The command ledger (`kd4-sinkhole-r4/commands.ndjson`, historical scratch artifact outside the repo) records each run's exact head and input hashes. The round-4 authored changes affect documentation only. Geometry, lines, bake and graph inputs are unchanged by publication. Earlier measurements below are historical and superseded under ruling 28.

Every table number comes from the seven final-runtime untraced eight-seed batches in the round-4 dataset (`kd4-sinkhole-r4/analysis.json`, outside the repo). Commands use committed `res://tools/traversal_line.gd`, fixed-fps 90, `--table=park --stage=4 --seeds=1,2,3,4,5,6,7,8`, one batch per pilot and `--floor`. The measuring tool SHA-256 is `37e98e4ac7e0a5d4141d97828f5834a083d3267e83a90efccc26248b69ec574a`.

Complete Sable seed-7 result dictionaries agree between the eight-seed batch, the {1,7} batch, solo and traced solo. Rivet, Kest and Tarn agree traced/untraced on every seed used for distinct-line qualification. The agreement receipts (`kd4-sinkhole-r4/agreement-sable.json`) and input hashes (`kd4-sinkhole-r4/input-equality.json`) qualify the current table.

Cells show median / applicable worst seed. Segment cells show three medians and the reported ranges. Airborne and length cells show median / minimum..maximum. Peak, flow, tier-3 and ride-fact cells show the minimum; arrival, quiet and anchor show the maximum. The airborne and tier-3 bands judge the median; their extremes are reported. Ruling 14 judges anchor gap from the maximum. Ruling 13 gives the floor its own arrival-and-stops row; other floor pilot metrics are reported only.

The floor reaches in at most 99.68 s against the most conservative derived cap, 126.72 s, twice the fastest pilot median. The table also lists the cap derived separately from every pilot's median. All 56 raw stop counts and terrain stop counts are zero. Pack stops are zero, so no solo attribution exemption is needed and no terrain stop is relabeled. The historical Tally lamp-swarm stop remains historical.

Distinct lines use every unfrozen stage tick of the qualified Rivet/Kest/Tarn captures. For each seed and each pair, both directed fractions within 8 m in 3D are below 0.5; the maximum across all 48 directed comparisons is 0.4386. These are captured paths, not waypoint polylines. Planar comparisons are also recorded in the dataset.

### Historical round-2 and round-3 qualification

Every line passes `drop_in`, `tube_mouth`, `great_room` and `landmark` in order. The pilot lines remain byte-identical to the round-1 baseline. Kest takes the channel's right wall and Tarn its left wall over the last six points.

Round 2 failed determinism qualification. The gate measured Sable seed 7 at 69.96 s and 6300 ticks in the {1,7} batch, versus 69.84 s and 6289 ticks alone, with ten differing result fields. Rivet, Kest and Tarn traced/untraced results also differed. Those measurements and the older tables are historical evidence, not current qualification.

The same gate reproduced one Tally seed-1 pack stop at [-621.2, -700.3], 7.10 s. Contact was lamp_swarm from deep_crew, placed by the director at 5.022 s through world.gd:505 and combat.gd:488. The saved equality receipt (`kd4-sinkhole-r2/provenance-review.json`, historical scratch artifact) shows complete result equality between the batch, plain solo and observer solo. Ruling 20 exempts this demonstrated enemy contact from the terrain dead-stop band. It does not repair the broader measuring-tool disagreement.

```text
traversal_line: tally seed 1 -> engaged, flow share 0.863, dead stops 1, anchor gap 14.0 m, to Warden 97.12 s, path 2057.8 m, rides ["kicker_launch", "ride_grapple_line", "ride_chute"], uphill 51.9 m
```

Round 3 merged candidate b1cc003, including TRAV-DET 9467de7, KE6 84b17e6 and REWARD-PARK 13e7967. The round-3 prerequisite failed again: Sable seed 7 takes 69.13 s and 6225 ticks in the {1,7} batch, versus 69.19 s and 6231 ticks alone. Ten result fields differ. The input-equality receipt (`kd4-sinkhole-r3/input-equality.json`) confirms identical stage inputs and unchanged runtime sources. The result comparison (`kd4-sinkhole-r3/sable-agreement.json`) lists every difference. Ruling 24 stopped that round's qualification and returned TRAV-DET to the coordinator. The seven eight-seed batches and distinct-lines captures were not started. That round produced no qualified table or floor cap. The round-4 table above supersedes it. No geometry or pilot line changes were made for the historical pack stop.

**Ride facts.** Runtime row sensors publish `ride_chute`, `ride_drop` and `ride_bowl`. The kickers and ropes add `kicker_launch` and `ride_grapple_line`. `lift_fired` is reported but excluded from the ride-fact band.

**Flow.** The 45 m cone gives the blazing tier before the channel. The channel leads through the cinder kicker, rope fan and crust kicker to the tube mouth. Each upper tube has a vent and kicker. Every route rejoins at the great-room camp, then a lower tube and its rope fan carry the ball toward the ring.

## 10. Music arc

The stage keeps its band: synth theme 1 and the ACE track `dig_deep` (`alpha_defs.gd:91`, `src/presentation/music_rows.gd:11`).

| Where or when | What the music does | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Cone rim, the first 3 s | wind and the bed only | the first downbeat is the first landing in the crater |
| Crater and breach | the groove enters on the landing; a fill on the breach jump | drop-in equals music drop |
| Lava channel | full groove; the fumarole launch is a 1-bar riser | the vent is a held breath |
| Tube mouth | the band drops under a low-pass filter (start: 1.2 kHz cut) as the roof closes over you | form mirrors function: under a roof the music is muffled too |
| Tubes | the filter opens for one beat under each skylight; `drip_keys` plays sparse notes in the dark | the light and the sound open together |
| Great room | the filter half-opens and the reverb widens to the cavern | a big room sounds big |
| Lower tubes | a 2-bar riser as the tube's end brightens | the sinkhole is the stage's big drop |
| Gallery ring | the filter opens fully on the downbeat you burst onto the ring; the light column's `landmark_hum` sits under the band | sky, sound and landmark arrive together |
| Warden engaged | the lamp guard's own sound, then the swarm's G5 buzz locking into D5 G5 C6 F6 on each pulse (`SOUND-STORY.md:60`); the swarm never enters the band (`SOUND-STORY.md:69`, `280`) | the enemy's music is its own sound |
| Each chunk lost | the pool rises one step with a low water swell, and the locked chord loses its top voice | progress you can hear and see |
| Break | the pool floods to the ring; the chord breaks into single detuned notes and stops; the band's next downbeat is the drop | the drop lands on the break |
| Events | cave-in: low sub riser into a rock hit; barge: Brindle's anvil and brass (PARK.md section 5 rule 4); eruption: the swarm buzz rising; drain: a falling gurgle; boiler: the steam whistle into a percussion hit | one family, one sound |
| Weather layers | `drip_keys` always; `rain_toms` while the monsoon lives | weather changes the view and the sound, never the handling |

## 11. Codex fragments

Short field notes in the deep works' existing voice (`src/core/lore_rows.gd:195-224`). Ids are new; the KD4 unit adds them.

| Id | Found at | Text (draft) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `ref_granary_ledge` | `trench_granaries` | Small stone rooms set high in the trench wall, doors no wider than a shoulder. Built to keep seed dry through a bad year. |
| `lava_cone_note` | `cinder_cone` | Cone survey: crater cold. Rock under the crater, not cold. |
| `lava_tube_flow` | `tube_mouth` | The tubes ran lava downhill, then nothing for a long time. The floors are polished anyway. |
| `lava_cenote_note` | `west_gallery_1` | Flooded gallery: water clear to the bottom. The lights are not on the bottom. |
| `mining_drift_tally` | `east_drift_1` | Drift tally: timber sets every six metres. One more set going in than coming out. |
| `ancient_buried_lens` | `machine_vault` | Vault seam: green, steady, warm to the glove. The seam is a lid. |
| `mining_cap_stencil` | `great_room_cap` | Cap stencil: DO NOT SLAM. Bolts already loosened. From underneath. |
| `mining_haulage_rails` | `east_haulage_1` | Haulage rails: ore cars ran to the pump shaft. Rails polished. No cars. |
| `mining_gallery_timber` | `gallery_ring` | Gallery ring set at the hundred-metre mark. The brood has built a second ring inside it. |
| `mining_pump_caisson` | `caisson_bowl` | Caisson log: pumped dry at shift change. Water back by noon. Level rises when something breaks. |
| `mining_pump_ticket` | `pump_gallery` | Pump ticket: boiler rated for the caisson. Caisson rated for water. |
| `ancient_brood_count` | landmark | Brood count: every bolt in the deep works, sorted by size. The count is still going. |
| `ancient_polish_band` | `main_tube_upper_1` | Wall survey: polish band two metres up both walls, both directions. Lava only runs one way. |
| `ancient_vent_mouths` | `cannon_tube_2` | Vent survey: the mouths open on the polish band, never the floor. Nobody on this crew set them there. |
| `mining_wall_rule` | `main_tube_lower_1` | Shift rule, lower tubes: walk the wall. Floor's been taking boots. |
| `mining_rope_rings` | `main_tube_lower_4` | Rigging note: rings go up on the band. Nobody hangs a rope over a floor that bites. |

The `ref_` fragment describes the work and speaks for no living people (PARK.md section 4). `mining_pump_caisson` foreshadows the Warden's mechanism, and `ancient_brood_count` picks up the deep works' existing brood lines (`lore_rows.gd:215`, `224`). The existing codex entries this stage teaches stay: `lure_bloom`, `bore_breach`, `burrow_lane`, `awaiting_operator` and the variant's `brood_dark` (PARK.md:431).

## 13. Storyboard frames

Board id `park-4-park_sinkhole`. PARK.md section 6.1 panels in brackets. The pilot ball, where shown, is the expanded Hoberman-sphere mecha (`docs/decisions.tsv:417`). Frames match the shipped greybox (KD4, 2026-10-11): the drop-in is on the cinder cone's north-west flank at (-690, -760), the cone stands 45 m, and the great room hosts the on-line camp.

1. **Drop-in vista [p2_drop_in].** Behind the ball high on the cinder cone's north-west flank in afternoon light, looking south-east. The lava channel's black half-pipe leaves the cone and curls across the basalt plateau. Beyond, a line of skylights pocks the plateau, and far south-east a thin uranium-glass green column rises out of it where the sinkhole is. A monsoon cell builds in the south-west sky.
2. **First flow line [p3_line].** Chase camera low in the lava channel, a half-pipe of black cinder, the ball high on the left wall. The banks guide the exit; the inner stamped wall at Sable's turn has moved 8.023 m outward. Ahead, the cinder kicker's lip and, past it, four ropes slung across the channel; the fumarole steams on the bank, and the tube mouth gapes at the channel's end.
3. **An enemy home [p4_home].** The flooded west gallery: a dark lava tube with still water along its floor, three lamp-swarm lures hanging over it as the only light. The Rivet ball cuts a straight wake through the middle at speed, the lures swinging toward where it was.
4. **The great room camp [p5_event, swapped].** The cavern junction where every tube meets: basalt pillars ringing the room, the camp's burrowers, drill crew and lamps round the floor, the ball banking through them toward a lower tube mouth while a vent behind it throws the next ball into the air.
5. **The landmark approach [p3_line, second].** From inside a lower tube, the round mouth ahead blazes with daylight. Through it, the sinkhole's far wall in banded basalt and limestone, the timber gallery ring, and the brood's green nest. The ball is a silhouette against the light.
6. **The Warden fight [p6_warden].** Wide shot from the gallery ring. The brood nest, a lumpy honeycomb of hexagonal cells, clings to the south wall over a round pool. The lamp guard circles over the water. A ball is mid-ram across the pool floor. Waterfall threads from the monsoon fall down the walls; the light column rises from the nest to the sky.
7. **The drop finisher [p6_warden, second].** Stagger, the pool raised two steps: the nest's shutters are blown open and the queen cell swells green. The ball falls from a harpoon swing straight onto it, the rope trailing.
8. **The exit.** The pool flooded to the gallery ring, flat and bright, the nest dark and dripping. The bay's hover barge docks over the water by the gate, and the launch arc rises up the sinkhole's open throat toward the sky and the next world.

The west roof cave-in, frame 4 of the draft, announces at clock tier 1 and lands after its 6 s warning on a bar when capacity, route and hold-clear rules allow it. Its site is off every measured line; it stays a roaming player's event and a codex frame, not a line frame.

## 14. Calls, veto list and open questions

**Calls made under the creative charge (veto list).**

1. The great room is the one junction every line passes; it is waymark 3 and hosts the on-line camp `great_room_camp` (KD4, R2).
2. Five upper routes (main tube, skylight chain, flooded gallery, east drift, cannon tube) give each pilot family its own way; the floor line takes the main tubes.
3. The pool rises 5 m a step from -120 to -105, and the break floods it to the gallery ring at -100.
4. The light column is 4 m wide and green. It is the stage's sight line, because the sinkhole cannot be seen from the plateau.
5. Skylights get no light shaft or pool, following the wave 11 lamp veto.
6. The ambush altar gives way to a hauler bounty, `east_chamber_bounty`, in a side chamber off the great room (KD4, R1, R7).
7. The Ancestral Puebloan granaries sit off every measured line, on the west loop, and host nothing hostile.
8. The music filter follows the roof: muffled under a roof, open under sky.
9. The hive cells home reinforces from the director's ancient pack, so no new pack id is needed.

**Open questions for K1, KD4 and the coordinator.**

1. Same schema gaps as stage 3: rescue rows, vault, relic spot and breach caps (stage 3 page, section 14, question 1). `map_dig_deep.gd:58` places today's relic spot south of Kest's cage; KD4 confirms whether stage 4 keeps a rescue at all.
2. Tube rows need a `y` profile along their length (mouth -12 m to ring -100 m). This draft uses one `y` per segment; the bake may want `y0` and `y1`.
3. Roofed cells: the sight test excludes them (PARK.md:474), but the schema has no roof flag; `tunnel` and `flooded_cavern` rows could imply it.
4. KE-FORMS supplies `crust_crack` -> `crust_crack_landed` and `drain_gurgle` -> `drain_landed`. The shared WORLD_ROWS records drive sight and sound, and the scheduler maps each landing through PARK_LAND_FACTS. Section 5 records both rows' real-stage telegraphs and landings, the passing drain effect and the remaining shared cave-in effect failure. Music cues `drop_cave_in` and `drop_drain` remain the authored KE8 cues.
5. Whether a lamp-swarm guard's scatter pays `GUARD_RACK_POSTURE` (`warden.gd:194-203` checks `enemy.get("swarm")` and `guard.following()`, which a lamp guard may not expose) is unverified; the kind sheet's section 3 asks KF3 to check it.

**KD4 greybox calls (2026-10-11), on the veto list.**

1. Waymark 3 is the landform row `great_room` (was the activity row `bounty_great_room`), and the on-line camp `great_room_camp` (was `cenote_camp`, at the west cenote) sits on it (R2, R3: the camp stood inside the `cenote_drain` site, 33 m off the line). Call 1 and call 6 above now read: the great room hosts the camp; the hauler bounty moved to a side chamber.
2. The hauler bounty is `east_chamber_bounty` (was `bounty_great_room`), in a side chamber off the great room's east wall, door 164 m of path from the line (the risk pocket); the cracked cache is `west_chamber_cache` (was `trench_cache`, in the west trench) in the west side chamber, 224 m off (the reward pocket); fix-round graph, 2026-10-12. PARK.md 2.12 wins over the journey beat (R7).
3. `sealed_crosscut` stands on its own `crosscut_drift` site, door at (-84, 150), clear of the `west_tube_roof` cave-in ring; its door is 79 m of path off the line (fix-round graph) and 238 m from the nearest door (R3).
4. The cinder cone stands 45 m (draft 15 m) and every tube carries a kicker, a lava vent on its straightest stretch and, in the lower tubes, a four-rope fan. Without them a pilot that cannot boost settled near 23 m/s on the 30 m tilt and missed the peak-speed, blazing-time and airborne bands.
5. Every tube is lined with basalt pillars and walls standing 16 to 20 m over the floor (posts, fillers and lip pillars) so the anchor probe never runs 40 m dry, even at a vent's apex.
6. Cross-stage fact 2 (one gate slot in world 2 against two arches) is reported, not changed.
7. The lines share the lava channel for nearly half their length, so Kest rides its right wall where it runs straight and Tarn its left wall over the channel's last six points, from (-190, -479) to the tube mouth (fix round: Rivet's left-wall points cost it about 3 s over the first segment once the posts were built, a 45.5 s median against the 45 s band, so Rivet now rides the middle). On the lines file's polylines Rivet, Kest and Tarn are mutually distinct (at most 0.41 of a line's 2 m samples within 8 m of another's). Offsetting Sable too put it into the channel's edge posts at the bends, so Sable keeps the middle.
8. Traced dead stops removed by moving rows, not bands: the tube-mouth fork pillar `main_tube_upper_fill_25` to (17, -309) and the bend post `main_tube_upper_post_2i` to (30, -305.5), off the inside of Sable's line; `west_breakthrough_10` shortened to 20 m at (-45, 283.5), so a ball leaving the main lower tube onto the ring lands on flat ground, not across that wash's bank.
9. Historical win proof (fix round, 2026-10-12), superseded by round 4 below: the completionist bot breaks the swarm brood and clears the stage on all three seeds (`bot_run.gd --policy=completionist --seeds=1,2,3 --table=park --from-stage=4 --stages=4`): engaged at 122.2, 125.1 and 113.0 s, broken at 166.8, 181.9 and 172.5 s, outcome `stages_done`. Before the fix it stalled at its 240 s limit because its straight approach from the great room wedged on the filler island at the ring (0, 255) for two minutes; those fillers are gone (section 4). It enters all three pockets and finishes only the camp: `tools/bot_driver.gd:178` gives each pocket 40 s from the first frame it is the target, and the bot targets the cache from the drop-in, 760 m away (traced seed 2: `activity cracked_cache runup` from t=2 s at (-667, -760), dropped at t=41 s at (-224, -552)), then chases the bounty's brute from the upper tubes until t=80 s. The pockets are reachable (graph routes `east_chamber_spur` and `west_chamber_spur` connected); the bot's clock, not the chambers, ends them. The skipper bot also breaks the brood on all three seeds (greybox round).
10. Round 2 and round 3 failed determinism qualification. Section 9 records the historical Tally pack contact and the qualified round-4 replacement measurements. Earlier tables are historical evidence; dressing remains separate.

### Current Warden proof, round 4

Completionist seeds 1 to 3 use three fresh processes on `--table=park --from-stage=4 --stages=4`, with the round-folder wrapper setting stage_limit to 600 s before driver ticks. All three reach and break the mounted swarm brood, then clear the stage. The table and proof use the same final runtime.

| Seed | Engage, s | Break, s | Fight elapsed, s | Outcome |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | 122.61 | 346.05 | 223.44 | stages_done at stage 5 |
| 2 | 120.50 | 391.22 | 270.72 | stages_done at stage 5 |
| 3 | 116.01 | 233.42 | 117.41 | stages_done at stage 5 |

```text
bot_run: completionist seed 1 (runner_gunner) -> stages_done at stage 5, 33529 ticks (372.5 s game), 377.3 s wall
bot_run: completionist seed 2 (demolitions_caster) -> stages_done at stage 5, 38510 ticks (427.9 s game), 371.7 s wall
bot_run: completionist seed 3 (tarn) -> stages_done at stage 5, 23155 ticks (257.3 s game), 141.4 s wall
```

The measured outcomes (`kd4-sinkhole-r4/analysis.json`, historical scratch artifact) and each seed's bots.ndjson supply engage, break and fight duration. No get_rid script error occurs. All three current seeds enter the cache, bounty and camp but finish only `enemy_camp`. This matches the known completionist limit: its pocket clock starts at the drop-in. The chamber routes remain connected in the graph; no timer or pocket workaround is made. Warden duration belongs to WT-PARK under ruling 23; this stage does not tune it. The cave-in effect failure in section 5 still blocks complete event acceptance.

 "home_rows": [
  {"id": "cinder_burrowers", "enemy": "burrower", "site": "lava_channel_1", "landform": "lava_tube", "advantage": "Loose black cinder fills the channel floor, so the burrow bulge hides in the grain and every line rides this channel in the first 30 s.", "counter": "The channel is a half-pipe: ride the wall above the committed 2.2 m lane (burrower.gd:10), or slam the marker; the codex burrow_lane pays for the side roll.", "reinforce": {"from_tier": 0, "every_s": 24.0, "max_alive": 3, "pack": "deep_bore"}, "break": {"by": "slam", "hp": 60, "scrap": 12}, "stops_kind": true, "dormant": false},
  {"id": "gallery_lamps", "enemy": "lamp_swarm", "site": "west_gallery_1", "landform": "cenote_pool", "advantage": "The flooded gallery is the darkest place in the park, so the lure bloom is the only light and it bends a slow ball toward the pool edge.", "counter": "Rivet's line sprints the gallery at cruise or faster, which the lure never touches (lamp_swarm.gd:7-9); a cruise ram scatters the lamps.", "reinforce": {"from_tier": 1, "every_s": 22.0, "max_alive": 3, "pack": "deep_lamps"}, "break": {"by": "ram", "hp": 50, "scrap": 10}, "stops_kind": true, "dormant": false},
  {"id": "drift_drill_crews", "enemy": "drill_crew", "site": "east_drift_3", "landform": "adit", "advantage": "The drift is narrow and straight, so a rig leading the ball by 0.8 s (drill_crew.gd:11) puts its 3 m ring dead on the line.", "counter": "The timber sets every 6 m are claw and harpoon anchors: swing over the ring, or smash the rig mid-bore.", "reinforce": {"from_tier": 1, "every_s": 26.0, "max_alive": 2, "pack": "deep_crew"}, "break": {"by": "smash", "hp": 80, "scrap": 14}, "stops_kind": false, "dormant": false},
  {"id": "hive_cells", "enemy": "swarm_remnant", "site": "gallery_ring", "landform": "sinkhole", "advantage": "The brood's outer cells line the gallery ring above the caisson, so drones dive from the ring onto a ball pumping the bowl below.", "counter": "Bowl through a forming rack at speed and the drones follow you; lead them into the lamp guard as pins (codex awaiting_operator).", "reinforce": {"from_tier": 1, "every_s": 30.0, "max_alive": 6, "pack": "swarm_remnant"}, "break": {"by": "smash", "hp": 90, "scrap": 16}, "stops_kind": true, "dormant": false},
  {"id": "barge_bore_crew", "enemy": "drill_crew", "site": "bore_apron", "landform": "lava_tube", "advantage": "The barge bores straight down into the east haulage, so its crew arrives under the plateau ahead of a ball already in the tubes.", "counter": "The bore shaft it opens is a drop onto the haulage rails; ride it down past the crew, or shoot the mast from the rim.", "reinforce": {"from_tier": 2, "every_s": 30.0, "max_alive": 2, "pack": "deep_crew"}, "break": {"by": "damage", "hp": 150, "scrap": 20}, "stops_kind": true, "dormant": true}
 ],
